```
Links: [{"title":"claude sonnet 5.5","url":"https://openrouter.ai/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5"},{"title":"claude sonnet 5.5","url":"https://openrouter.ai/models/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5"},{"title":"claude sonnet 5 5","url":"https://openrouter.ai/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5"},{"title":"claude sonnet 5.5 20260928","url":"https://openrouter.ai/models/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5-20260928"},{"title":"claude sonnet 5.5 20260928","url":"https://openrouter.ai/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5-20260928"},{"title":"claude sonnet 5.5","url":"https://www.typingmind.com/guide/openrouter/claude-sonnet-5.5"},{"title":"claude sonnet 5.5","url":"https://openrouter.ai/compare/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5"},{"title":"Claude Sonnet 5.5 on OpenRouter","url":"https://www.llmreference.com/model/claude-sonnet-5-5/openrouter"},{"title":"Using Claude Sonnet 5.5 on OpenRouter","url":"https://www.llmreference.com/provider/openrouter/claude-sonnet-5-5"}]

The OpenRouter model ID is `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`. Its OpenRouter listing reports a 1M-token context window, and the sources disagree on pricing.

- **Overview and release:** OpenRouter describes it as Anthropic's Sonnet-class model for everyday work, positioned as an upgrade over Claude Sonnet 5. It was released on September 28, 2026.
- **Providers:** It is served by 5 providers on OpenRouter: Google Vertex, Amazon Bedrock, Azure, Claude Platform on AWS, and Anthropic. OpenRouter routes requests to the best available provider, with automatic failover to the others, and you can pin or exclude providers.
- **Capabilities:** It accepts text, images, and files such as PDFs as input and returns text. It accepts tools and tool_choice for function calling and supports structured JSON output. Its listing also notes that it supports up to 128,000 completion tokens.
- **Reasoning behavior:** Thinking is always on, so effort is the main lever for trading off depth, latency, and cost.
- **Pricing (conflicting):** The OpenRouter page shows $1 input / $5 output per million tokens. A third-party guide (TypingMind) and LLMReference both list $2.00 input $10.00 output $0.20 cache read per million tokens. Check the live OpenRouter pricing page before budgeting.
- **Naming caveat:** LLMReference notes that the provider model ID is "anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5", not the LLMReference slug "claude-sonnet-5-5". The Anthropic API ID is different; LLMReference lists it as claude-sonnet-5-5.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```
OpenRouter documents a path for Claude Code and the Anthropic SDKs that uses its Anthropic-compatible Messages endpoint, and the setup is mostly environment variables.

**Claude Code setup**
- Claude Code's base URL is the bare host path, `https://openrouter.ai/api`, with no `/v1`. Their guide says that adding `/v1` produces model-not-found errors. The base URL is https://openrouter.ai/api — **no /v1 suffix**. This is OpenRouter's Anthropic Skin, which speaks the native Anthropic protocol.
- The auth token holds your OpenRouter key, and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` must be set to an empty string. If it's unset, Claude Code can fall back to authenticating against Anthropic directly.
- To check the connection, run `/status` in Claude Code. It should list `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` as the auth source and the OpenRouter URL as the base URL. If the Auth token line names ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, or a Login method line names a Claude account instead, the environment did not reach the session — reload your shell and relaunch claude.
- No proxy is needed. When you set ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL to https://openrouter.ai/api, Claude Code speaks its native protocol directly to OpenRouter. No local proxy server is required.

**Anthropic Messages endpoint**
- The raw endpoint is `/api/v1/messages`. Its docs show a POST request with an Anthropic-style body (`max_tokens`, `messages`, `model`) and a Bearer token header, using a model slug such as `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4`. The docs show fetch calls to https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/messages with the model set to anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.
- The Anthropic TypeScript SDK can be pointed at OpenRouter by setting its base URL to `https://openrouter.ai/api` and passing the OpenRouter key as the API key. The docs show this with `anthropic.beta.messages.create`, and the example also demonstrates a `fallbacks` parameter. The Anthropic Messages API endpoint (/api/v1/messages) accepts the fallbacks parameter, matching the shape used by Anthropic's SDKs.

**Agent SDK**
- The Claude Agent SDK runs on Claude Code, so it uses the same variables. Since the Agent SDK uses Claude Code as its runtime, you can connect it to OpenRouter using the same environment variables.

**Limits**
- OpenRouter says the Claude Code integration is built for Anthropic models. It is only guaranteed to work with the Anthropic first-party provider. Claude Code expects Anthropic request semantics, so non-Anthropic models aren't supported through the native endpoint.
- For non-Claude tools, OpenRouter's OpenAI-compatible
```

<details><summary>🔧 WebFetch</summary>

```json
{
  "url": "https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/features/structured-outputs",
  "prompt": "Summarize exactly: request format for structured outputs (response_format json_schema, strict), which models/providers support it (Anthropic/Claude?), does it work with streaming, any limitations, and the require_parameters provider option. Quote key sentences."
}
```

Result:

```
**Request format:** Add a `response_format` object to your chat completion request with `type` set to `"json_schema"`. The `json_schema` object contains a `name`, a `strict` flag, and your `schema`. The page's example asks for a weather object with `location`, `temperature`, and `conditions`, and sets `additionalProperties: false`.

**Strict mode:** Setting `strict: true` asks providers with a native strict mode to enforce your schema exactly. Enforcement varies. As the page puts it, "exact compliance is not guaranteed on every endpoint." Strict modes may also limit which JSON Schema features you can use.

**Model and provider support:** Support is limited to select models, and it's determined per endpoint. The page says "Support is determined per endpoint, not just per model," so the same model may work through one provider but not another. To check, use the structured outputs filter on the models page or the `structured_outputs` parameter in a model's Providers section. The page links to provider docs from OpenAI, Google Gemini, Anthropic, and Fireworks, but it doesn't say which Claude models support structured outputs, so check the models page for that.

**Streaming:** Yes. Add `stream: true` to the request. Per the page, "The model will stream valid partial JSON that, when complete, forms a valid response matching your schema."

**Limitations:**
- Unsupported models fail with an error.
- An invalid schema causes the model to return an error.
- Endpoint support can change over time.
- Strict mode may restrict schema features.
- Strict compliance varies by provider.
- The Response Healing plugin is available for non-streaming requests with `type: "json_schema"` to reduce invalid JSON.

**`require_parameters` option:** To route requests only to endpoints that support your parameters, set `require_parameters: true` in your provider preferences. Also include `response_format` with `type: json_schema` in the required parameters. The page refers to Provider Routing for the details of this setting.
```

</details>

<details><summary>🔧 WebFetch</summary>

```json
{
```
```json
  "url": "https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-go/option",
  "prompt": "List the exported option functions relevant to: base URL override, API key, auth token / bearer auth, extra JSON body fields (e.g. WithJSONSet), extra headers, max retries, request timeout. Give exact function names and signatures."
}
```

Result:

```
**Base URL override**
- `func WithBaseURL(base string) RequestOption`

**API key**
- `func WithAPIKey(value string) RequestOption`

**Auth token / bearer auth**
- `func WithAuthToken(value string) RequestOption`

**Extra JSON body fields**
- `func WithJSONSet(key string, value any) RequestOption`: the key uses the sjson format.
- `func WithJSONDel(key string) RequestOption`: removes a body field.

**Extra headers**
- `func WithHeader(key, value string) RequestOption`: overwrites any existing value.
- `func WithHeaderAdd(key, value string) RequestOption`: appends to existing values.
- `func WithHeaderDel(key string) RequestOption`: removes the header.

**Max retries**
- `func WithMaxRetries(retries int) RequestOption`: the default is two retries. "WithMaxRetries panics when retries is negative."

**Request timeout**
- `func WithRequestTimeout(dur time.Duration) RequestOption`: applies to each attempt, not the whole call.
```

</details>

```
  whether the API accepts the 12-variant schema.
```

```
## D8 — Claude structured output: `output_config.format`

- **Options:**
  - **A.** Ask for JSON in the prompt and parse it (fragile, no guarantee).
  - **B.** Strict tool use (`strict: true`).
  - **C.** Native Structured Outputs (`output_config.format` with a JSON schema).
- **Decision:** C, with B as fallback.
- **Why:** With C, the API constrains the response to the schema and it works with streaming. For
  B: on current Opus/Sonnet models a forced `tool_choice` (`any`/`tool`) returns 400, so B needs
  `auto` plus prompting plus a "did it actually call the tool?" check. That is more moving parts.
- **To verify (P3):** the Go SDK binding for `output_config.format` is not in the docs we checked.
  We will confirm it against the SDK source. If it is missing, we fall back to B, which is
  documented for Go.
- **Segment schema (built in P2):** `segment.RuleSchema` generates the rule's schema from the
  registry. It has 12 `anyOf` variants, one per (field, operator group), so incompatible
  combinations and unknown cities are excluded at generation time. Whether the real API accepts a
  schema of this size is verified in P3; if it does not, we fall back to a single generic
  condition schema, and the validator remains the real guarantee either way.
- **Schema limits that shape the design:**
  - No recursive schemas → the DSL is flat (D9).
  - No `minLength`/`maxLength` or `minimum`/`maximum` → character limits and numeric ranges
    **cannot** be enforced by the schema and are enforced by the backend (D10).

## D9 — Segment rule: flat, whitelisted JSON DSL
## D17 — Frontend stack

- **Decision:** Vue 3 (Composition API) + Vite + TypeScript, served as a static build by nginx,
  which also proxies `/api` (SSE unbuffered).
- **Component library:** Naive UI or PrimeVue, decided at the start of P6.
```

```
- **Decision:** `llm.Client` interface with two implementations: `anthropic` (the official
  Anthropic Go SDK) and `mock`.
  - The provider is chosen by `LLM_PROVIDER=auto|anthropic|mock`: `auto` uses Anthropic when
    `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set and the mock otherwise.
  - `LLM_MODEL` overrides the default model (D13).
  - The API reports which mode is active so the UI can show a demo/mock badge.
- **Client construction is explicit:** key, retries and timeouts are passed as SDK options
  (`option.WithAPIKey`, `WithMaxRetries`, `WithRequestTimeout`) from our config. We do not rely on
  the SDK silently picking up environment variables, so what runs is what the config says.
- **Why only the direct API:** the case requires the key to come from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and the
  developer uses the same key path as reviewers. An OpenRouter path was considered and dropped
  before P3.
```

```
## D13 — Model selection

- **Decision:** default `claude-opus-5-5`, configurable via `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`. `effort` is set
  explicitly per call (not left to the model default), tuned for latency in P3.
- **Note:** on this model thinking cannot be disabled; `effort` is the speed/depth control.
```
with:
```
## D13 — Model selection: Claude Sonnet 5.5

- **Decision:** Claude Sonnet 5.5 (`claude-sonnet-5-5`), configurable via `LLM_MODEL`.
  *(Revised before P3; the first draft defaulted to Opus 5.5.)*
- **Why:** both tasks (rule extraction and short copy) are interactive. The marketer waits for
  them, so latency and cost per draft matter more than maximum reasoning depth.
- **Model behaviour we design around:**
  - `effort` is set explicitly per call, not left to the model default (levels are recalibrated on
    this model); it is tuned for latency in P3.
  - `thinking: {type: "disabled"}` returns 400. If latency requires it, we use
    `{type: "between_tools"}` or a lower effort.
  - Forced `tool_choice` returns 400, consistent with structured outputs as the primary method (D8).
  - `stop_reason` of `refusal` or `max_tokens` is handled before any parsing (D10).
- **To evaluate in P3:** the server-side refusal fallback (`fallbacks: "default"` with the
  `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01` beta), so a refused request can still be answered.
```

```
## D22 — Design reference: visual only; adopted additions

- **Adopted from the design (API additions, P3):**
  - The generate request takes the brief, the selected channels (SMS/email) and a tone. The model
    also returns a short campaign name.
  - Per-channel draft states: `writing → validating → fixing (attempt n of 3) → valid /
    needs_manual_edit`. Three attempts in total = the D10 repair loop. SMS can be validated while
    email is still streaming.
  - Campaign statuses: Draft, Ready, Sending, Completed, Failed. Message states (P4): Queued,
    Sending, Sent, Retrying, Failed.
  - `Describe` uses the design's wording, e.g. "Added to cart in the last 30 days" and
    "No purchase in the last 30 days".
  - The API reports the LLM mode (mock or anthropic) for a demo badge.
```

```bash
cd "~/Desktop/Smart Campagin Composer Case" && sed -n '/^### P3 — LLM layer/,/^### P4/p' docs/PLAN.md; sed -n '/^### P4 —/,/^### P7/p' docs/PLAN.md | head -60; sed -n '/^## Risks/,/^## Target/p' docs/PLAN.md
```

Result:

```
### P3 — LLM layer
- **Goal:** brief → validated segment rule + validated SMS/email drafts, with or without an API key.
- **Deliverables:**
  - `internal/llm`: `Client` interface; `anthropic` implementation (structured outputs); `mock` implementation driven by a scenario catalog (`testdata/llm-scenarios/`: happy-path and fault scenarios, streaming).
  - Prompts + JSON schemas for segment and content; two-layer validation (schema shape, then business rules).
  - Repair loop: on validation failure, re-ask with the concrete errors (max 2 retries), then surface errors.
  - `internal/content`: variable whitelist, length rules (rune-based), worst-case length check, renderer.
  - Campaign draft endpoints + SSE stream for content generation (`draft.delta`, `draft.validated`, `draft.invalid`, `draft.repairing`).
  - Provider selection: `LLM_PROVIDER=auto|anthropic|mock`.
- **Done when:**
  - With the mock: sample brief → valid DSL → count → streamed, validated SMS + email.
  - Every fault scenario (broken JSON, unknown field, 200-char SMS, unknown variable, refusal, timeout) produces the expected repair-then-error behaviour — one test per scenario.
  - The real-key path is treated as the primary demo: prompts and error handling polished as if evaluators run with a key.
  - Manual smoke test with a real key; the real responses are saved as fixtures under `testdata/` and replayed in tests.
  - Go SDK binding for `output_config.format` verified against the SDK source (fallback: strict tool use).
- **Estimate:** 8 h. **Covers:** R1, R5 (API), R6, R7, R16, R17, U1.

### P4 — Approval & delivery pipeline
### P4 — Approval & delivery pipeline
- **Goal:** reliable, rate-limited, exactly-once-effect sending.
- **Deliverables:**
  - Campaign state machine; approval as a conditional update (idempotent).
  - Approval freezes the audience: `deliveries` rows inserted in the same transaction, `UNIQUE(campaign_id, user_id, channel)`.
  - Worker: claim with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` + lease (`locked_until`), per-channel rate limiter (100/s, burst 1), per-channel worker pool.
  - Retry policy: exponential backoff with full jitter for 5xx/timeouts/network errors; honour `Retry-After` on 429; permanent failure on other 4xx; max attempts → `failed`.
  - `Idempotency-Key: <delivery id>` on every provider call.
  - Mock provider: configurable latency, 5xx rate, 429 rate and its own rate limit via env; idempotency-key dedup.
- **Done when (integration tests against real Postgres):**
  - Approving twice creates one set of deliveries.
  - Provider accepts but the response times out → retry → provider records exactly one message.
  - In any half-open 1 s window, sends per channel ≤ 100.
  - Worker killed mid-campaign and restarted → every delivery ends exactly once, none lost.
  - Users without consent for a channel receive 0 messages on it.
- **Estimate:** 8 h. **Covers:** R4, R7 (render), R8 (API), R9, R10, R11, R12, R20.

### P5 — Tracking
- **Goal:** observable sending.
- **Deliverables:** `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/events` (SSE, periodic aggregate snapshots), `/metrics` (Prometheus), `/healthz` (liveness) + `/readyz` (DB ping).
- **Done when:** `curl -N` shows snapshots changing during a send; counters on `/metrics` increase; `/readyz` fails when Postgres is stopped.
- **Estimate:** 3 h. **Covers:** R13 (API), R14, R15.
… (truncated; 63 lines total)
```

</details>

Kararları dokümanlara işledim ve P3 prompt'unu güncelledim. Değişiklikler commit edilmedi; P3 session'ı ilk adımında tasarım dosyaları ve export ile birlikte commit edecek.

## Neler değişti

**`DECISIONS.md`**
- **D13 — model:** Artık Claude Sonnet 5.5 (`claude-sonnet-5-5`), doğrudan Anthropic API üzerinden. Seçme sebebi: brief'ten kural ve mesaj üretmek interaktif işler, pazarlamacı sonucu bekliyor; bu yüzden hız ve maliyet en derin akıl yürütmeden daha önemli. Bu modelin kısıtları da not edildi: thinking kapatılamıyor, zorunlu tool çağrısı (`tool_choice`) 400 dönüyor, `effort` her çağrıda açıkça verilecek. İsteği reddettiğinde başka modele düşme seçeneği P3'te değerlendirilecek.
- **D12 — LLM katmanı:** İki uygulama var: `anthropic` ve `mock`. SDK client'ı config'ten açıkça kuruluyor; SDK'nın kendi başına ortam değişkenlerini okumasına güvenmiyoruz. Örnek brief kütüphanesi de mock'un bir parçası. OpenRouter'ın değerlendirilip bırakıldığı kayıt altında.
- **D8 — structured output:** P3'te nasıl doğrulanacağı eklendi: gerçek API'de tek seferlik bir smoke test, yanıtlar fixture olarak kaydedilip testlerde tekrar kullanılacak.
- **D15 — içerik kuralları:** Tasarımla çelişkide bizim kuralımız geçerli. Arayüzdeki sayaç, backend'in kontrol ettiği sayıyı (en kötü durum uzunluğunu) gösterecek.
- **D22 — yeni:**
  - Tasarım sadece görsel referans.
  - Tasarımdan alınan API eklemeleri listelendi.
  - Senin onayladığın 4 özellik, hangi aşamada yapılacağıyla birlikte tabloya kondu.
  - Tasarımda olup kapsam dışı kalanlar: arama, bildirimler, ayarlar, Audiences ve Templates sayfaları.
- **D17:** Hazır UI kütüphanesi kararı P6'da yeniden değerlendirilecek. **Açık soru 1:** çözüldü olarak işaretlendi.

**`docs/PLAN.md`**
- **P3:** Tasarımdan gelen eklemeler, `docs/API.md` kontratı ve Sonnet 5.5 ile gerçek API smoke testi eklendi. Tahmin 9 saate çıktı.
- **P4:** Deneme/hata kayıtları (`delivery_attempts`) ve mesaj durum adları eklendi.
- **P5:** Saniyelik gönderim verisi ve son hatalar eklendi; "hiçbir saniyede 100'ü aşmıyor" artık bir "bitti" kriteri.
- **P6:** Tasarımın görsel referans olarak kullanımı ve yazı tiplerinin npm'den alınması eklendi. Tahmin 10–12 saat.
- **Toplam tahmin:** 44 saatten yaklaşık 47 saate çıktı.
- **Risk tablosuna iki satır eklendi:** geliştirme sırasındaki API harcaması ve tasarımın P6'yı taşırma ihtimali.

Hafızayı da güncelledim: Sonnet 5.5 ve Anthropic key kullanımı, OpenRouter'a dönülmeyeceği, tasarımın sadece görsel olduğu.

## Yeni P3 başlangıç prompt'u

```
## Amaç
Smart Campaign Composer take-home'unda P3'ü (LLM katmanı) başlat. Bu turda SADECE güncel durumu özetle ve P3'ün detaylı uygulama planını sun. Onayım olmadan kod yazma, dosya değiştirme.

## Önce oku (başka hiçbir şey yapmadan)
- docs/CASE.md; docs/PLAN.md (P3 bölümü ve Verification log); DECISIONS.md (özellikle D8, D9, D12–D15, D21, D22, Open questions)
- DESIGN.md ve design/screens/*.png (Read ile görüntüle): SADECE görsel referans. Ürün kuralları (değişkenler, uzunluk politikası, metinler) DECISIONS.md'den gelir.
- backend/internal/segment/ ve `git log --oneline -20`

## Bağlam (carry forward)
- P0–P2 tamam ve main'e merge edildi (PR #1, #2): Go 1.27 backend (api, worker, mockprovider, migrate, seed), docker compose stack, `make test` (container içinde, -race, gerçek Postgres), 5.000 deterministik seed kullanıcısı.
- Segment DSL hazır: katı parse → Validate → parametreli SQL; POST /api/segments/preview; RuleSchema (12 anyOf varyant). Örnek brief = 177 kişi / 99 SMS / 133 email.
- Model Claude Sonnet 5.5 (`claude-sonnet-5-5`), doğrudan Anthropic API üzerinden, resmi Go SDK ile. Key .env'deki ANTHROPIC_API_KEY'den okunur (D12, D13).
- Henüz commit edilmemiş: design/, DESIGN.md, ai-sessions/ altındaki ilk export, DECISIONS.md ve PLAN.md güncellemeleri.
- Teslim 2026-10-26. LLM entegrasyonu değerlendirmenin %25'i.

## P3'te mutlaka ele alınacaklar (ayrıntı PLAN.md P3'te)
- `LLM_PROVIDER=auto|anthropic|mock` (auto: key varsa anthropic, yoksa mock). Model `LLM_MODEL` ile değiştirilebilir. Client'ı açık option'larla kur; SDK'nın env varsayılanlarına güvenme.
- Gerçek API'de doğrula: Go SDK'da `output_config.format` desteği ve 12 varyantlı şemanın Sonnet 5.5 tarafından kabul edilmesi. Yedek: strict tool use veya genel şema. Yanıtları fixture olarak kaydet ve testlerde tekrar oynat.
- Sonnet 5.5 kısıtları: zorunlu (forced) tool_choice 400 verir, `thinking: disabled` 400 verir. effort her çağrıda açıkça set edilmeli. stop_reason refusal/max_tokens, parse'tan önce ele alınmalı. Server-side fallback'i değerlendir.
- Senaryo kataloğu mock'u ve ona bağlı örnek brief'ler (GET /api/briefs/examples).
- Üretim isteği: brief + kanallar + ton. Kısa bir kampanya adı üretilir.
- Kanal başına durumlar: writing → validating → fixing (attempt n/3) → valid / needs_manual_edit. Toplam 3 deneme. Email yazılırken SMS doğrulanabilmeli.
- İçerik validator'ı: rune bazlı SMS ≤ 160 / konu ≤ 80; {{first_name}}, {{last_name}}, {{city}}; kitledeki en uzun değerlerle en kötü durum uzunluğu. API bu en uzun değerleri ve hesaplanan uzunlukları döndürür.
- Önizleme iki şekilde: kurgusal bir örnek kullanıcıyla ve kitledeki en uzun isimle (en kötü durum). Preview yanıtında kanal başına consent nedeniyle dahil edilmeyenler ve toplam kanal mesajı da olmalı.
- Kampanya taslak tablosu. Durum adları: Draft, Ready, Sending, Completed, Failed. Describe metinleri tasarımdaki gibi olmalı. API mod bilgisini (mock / anthropic) döndürmeli.
- REST/SSE kontratını docs/API.md'de dokümante et.

## Bu turun çıktısı (Türkçe, bu sırayla)
1. Güncel durum özeti, en fazla 8 madde.
2. P3 adımları tablosu: her adım için ne yapılacak, doğrulama kanıtı (test, kasıtlı bozma ile mutation check, canlı curl) ve tahmini süre. İlk adım: commit edilmemiş dosyaları ai-sessions için PII grep kontrolünden sonra commit'lemek ve ai-sessions/README.md index'ini güncellemek.
3. Açık kararlar: her biri için 2–3 seçenek, trade-off'lar ve önerin.
4. Tek soru: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY .env'de hazır mı?
Sonra DUR ve onayımı bekle.

- Sadece istenen değişiklikleri yap. Ekstra özellik, soyutlama ya da dosya ekleme.
```
